People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care about people or anything, because I do.

But things I used to really love just don’t feel the same. Preaching used to feel… alive to me. Playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those were things that filled me up. Now I either avoid them or I just kind of do them because I’m supposed to.

I try to be polite about it, but I just don’t want to go there.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep sometimes. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nSome nights are fine enough, I guess, and other nights I’m just… not really settled. But I don’t know if I’d call it “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean. Is there a specific kind of sleep problem you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself jumping or being easily startled by loud noises or unexpected movements?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really paid attention to, anyway.\n\nI mean, loud noises aren’t my favorite, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m jumping out of my skin or anything. Maybe if something is really sudden, like anyone would react, but I haven’t thought of it as a major thing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know logically people tell me it wasn’t my fault. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with that.\n\nBut I do go over it a lot. I keep thinking if I had ended things earlier, or walked him out myself, or just kept a better eye on those boys… maybe he’d still be here.\n\nI know that may not be fair to say, but it’s hard not to feel like I missed something I was supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I’m worthless exactly. That sounds harsh, and I know I’m not supposed to talk about myself that way.\n\nBut most days there’s this… weight in my chest, like fear and anger and guilt all mixed together. From the time I wake up, I feel like I failed God, and my family, and the kids who trusted me. I know maybe that’s not the same as being a bad person, but it feels close sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to feel happy or loving feelings towards people or things you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care about people or anything, because I do.\n\nBut things I used to really love just don’t feel the same. Preaching used to feel… alive to me. Playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those were things that filled me up. Now I either avoid them or I just kind of do them because I’m supposed to.\n\nIt’s like I’m there, but I’m not really in it. I don’t know if that answers it right, but… yeah, it’s harder to feel that joy like I used to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Honestly… not really like before.

I mean, I still try sometimes. I’ll sit at the keys or show up around the guys, or I’ll do what I need to do with preaching, but it doesn’t feel alive in me the way it used to. Taking pictures, playing, being on the court… those used to give me something back.

I don’t know if I’d say I can’t feel anything at all… I mean, that sounds pretty extreme, and I don’t want to overstate it.

Does that make sense? Like… I know something is supposed to be good, but I don’t really feel it strong.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, thank you for seeing me. I guess I’ve been trying to handle it, and maybe it’s not as bad as I’m making it sound, but… I keep getting these moments where I’m just suddenly back there.\n\nIt’s the scene with my cousin on the pavement. I can hear the shouting again, see the lights, like it’s happening right in front of me—even if I’m just standing in the church office trying to act normal. It kind of hits me out of nowhere. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these sudden moments of being back at the scene with your cousin?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much since it happened. My cousin was killed about two years and three months ago now, and these moments started after that. I guess I kept thinking they’d fade if I just stayed busy, but they’re still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities because they remind you of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make a big deal out of it, but I guess I do.\n\nI’ll take the long way around town so I don’t have to pass the community center, or that court nearby. It probably sounds kind of silly, but if I’m already having a hard day, I just… I don’t want to see it.\n\nAnd if people start talking about that night, I usually shut it down pretty quickly or find some reason to step out. I try to be polite about it, but I just don’t want to go there.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep sometimes. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nSome nights are fine enough, I guess, and other nights I’m just… not really settled. But I don’t know if I’d call it “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean. Is there a specific kind of sleep problem you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself jumping or being easily startled by loud noises or unexpected movements?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really paid attention to, anyway.\n\nI mean, loud noises aren’t my favorite, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m jumping out of my skin or anything. Maybe if something is really sudden, like anyone would react, but I haven’t thought of it as a major thing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know logically people tell me it wasn’t my fault. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with that.\n\nBut I do go over it a lot. I keep thinking if I had ended things earlier, or walked him out myself, or just kept a better eye on those boys… maybe he’d still be here.\n\nI know that may not be fair to say, but it’s hard not to feel like I missed something I was supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I’m worthless exactly. That sounds harsh, and I know I’m not supposed to talk about myself that way.\n\nBut most days there’s this… weight in my chest, like fear and anger and guilt all mixed together. From the time I wake up, I feel like I failed God, and my family, and the kids who trusted me. I know maybe that’s not the same as being a bad person, but it feels close sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to feel happy or loving feelings towards people or things you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if that answers it right, but… yeah, it’s harder to feel that joy like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your cousin's death that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not really like before.\n\nI mean, I still try sometimes. I’ll sit at the keys or show up around the guys, or I’ll do what I need to do with preaching, but it doesn’t feel alive in me the way it used to. Taking pictures, playing, being on the court… those used to give me something back.\n\nNow a lot of times I either avoid it, or I’m there but I’m just kind of going through the motions. I wish I could say there’s one thing that still feels the same, but… I don’t know. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to feel any strong emotions at all, even when something good happens?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I can’t feel anything at all… I mean, that sounds pretty extreme, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut even when something should feel good, it’s like it doesn’t really reach me the way it used to. Preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those used to really stir something in me. Now I mostly just go through the motions, or I avoid it altogether, because it doesn’t feel alive in me anymore.\n\nDoes that make sense? Like… I know something is supposed to be good, but I don’t really feel it strong.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say completely with everything, but there are things I’ve pretty much stopped doing unless I have to.

with the traumatic event.\n  manifestation: I take the long way around town so I don't pass the community center or that court, and if somebody starts talking about that night I shut it down or find a reason to leave.\n- key: S073\n  name: Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame\n  description: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.\n  manifestation: I keep telling myself that if I had ended the event earlier, walked him out, or kept better watch over those boys, my cousin would still be here.\n- key: S074\n  name: Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State\n  description: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).\n  manifestation: Most days I feel this mix of fear, anger, and guilt sitting in my chest from the time I wake up, like I failed God, my family, and the kids who trusted me.\n- key: S075\n  name: Social_Detachment\n  description: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.\n  manifestation: Even when I'm surrounded by people at church calling me 'brother' and asking me to pray, I feel like I'm behind glass and nobody can really reach me.\n- key: S077\n  name: Irritability_Aggression\n  description: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.\n  manifestation: I've snapped in ministry meetings over small things, raising my voice at deacons or youth leaders like they're the enemy, and afterward I hate how fast I went there.\n- key: S078\n  name: Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior\n  description: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.\n  manifestation: Some nights I drive around the blocks near the center looking for the faces I think might be trouble, and I've gotten out of the car to confront people even though I know it could get me shot too.\n- key: S079\n  name: Hypervigilance\n  description: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.\n  manifestation: I can't sit with my back to a door anymore, and at church events I'm always watching hands, corners, cars slowing down, and where the nearest exit is instead of paying attention to the people in front of me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional completely stopped doing since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say completely with everything, but there are things I’ve pretty much stopped doing unless I have to.\n\nLike taking pictures — I don’t really pick up the camera the way I used to. And playing keys… I might sit down sometimes, but it’s not like before. Same with getting on the court with the guys. I’ll tell myself I’m busy or just not show up.\n\nPreaching is harder to say because I still have responsibilities, but even there, a lot of the time I’m just going through the motions. None of it really feels alive in me anymore. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've significantly reduced or lost interest in since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve just quit everything, because I still try to show up where I’m supposed to.\n\nBut preaching used to mean a lot to me, and playing keys too. Taking pictures, running with the guys on the court… those were things I actually looked forward to. Since my cousin died, I’ve pulled back from a lot of it. Or if I do it, I’m kind of just there, going through the motions.\n\nIt just doesn’t feel alive in me like it used to. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to find very enjoyable that you've lost most of your interest in since your cousin passed away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that’s fair to say.\n\nI used to really love preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, and getting out on the court with the guys. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care about anything, but it’s just not there the same way anymore. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\n[Last Question]\nHow long have you been feeling guilty or blaming yourself for what happened to your cousin?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, thank you for seeing me. I guess I’ve been trying to handle it, and maybe it’s not as bad as I’m making it sound, but… I keep getting these moments where I’m just suddenly back there.\n\nIt’s the scene with my cousin on the pavement. I can hear the shouting again, see the lights, like it’s happening right in front of me—even if I’m just standing in the church office trying to act normal. It kind of hits me out of nowhere. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these sudden moments of being back at the scene with your cousin?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much since it happened. My cousin was killed about two years and three months ago now, and these moments started after that. I guess I kept thinking they’d fade if I just stayed busy, but they’re still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities because they remind you of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make a big deal out of it, but I guess I do.\n\nI’ll take the long way around town so I don’t have to pass the community center, or that court nearby. It probably sounds kind of silly, but if I’m already having a hard day, I just… I don’t want to see it.\n\nAnd if people start talking about that night, I usually shut it down pretty quickly or find some reason to step out. I try to be polite about it, but I just don’t want to go there.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep sometimes. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nSome nights are fine enough, I guess, and other nights I’m just… not really settled. But I don’t know if I’d call it “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean. Is there a specific kind of sleep problem you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself jumping or being easily startled by loud noises or unexpected movements?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really paid attention to, anyway.\n\nI mean, loud noises aren’t my favorite, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m jumping out of my skin or anything. Maybe if something is really sudden, like anyone would react, but I haven’t thought of it as a major thing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know logically people tell me it wasn’t my fault. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with that.\n\nBut I do go over it a lot. I keep thinking if I had ended things earlier, or walked him out myself, or just kept a better eye on those boys… maybe he’d still be here.\n\nI know that may not be fair to say, but it’s hard not to feel like I missed something I was supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I’m worthless exactly. That sounds harsh, and I know I’m not supposed to talk about myself that way.\n\nBut most days there’s this… weight in my chest, like fear and anger and guilt all mixed together. From the time I wake up, I feel like I failed God, and my family, and the kids who trusted me. I know maybe that’s not the same as being a bad person, but it feels close sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to feel happy or loving feelings towards people or things you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care about people or anything, because I do.\n\nBut things I used to really love just don’t feel the same. Preaching used to feel… alive to me. Playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those were things that filled me up. Now I either avoid them or I just kind of do them because I’m supposed to.\n\nIt’s like I’m there, but I’m not really in it. I don’t know if that answers it right, but… yeah, it’s harder to feel that joy like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your cousin's death that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not really like before.\n\nI mean, I still try sometimes. I’ll sit at the keys or show up around the guys, or I’ll do what I need to do with preaching, but it doesn’t feel alive in me the way it used to. Taking pictures, playing, being on the court… those used to give me something back.\n\nNow a lot of times I either avoid it, or I’m there but I’m just kind of going through the motions. I wish I could say there’s one thing that still feels the same, but… I don’t know. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to feel any strong emotions at all, even when something good happens?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I can’t feel anything at all… I mean, that sounds pretty extreme, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut even when something should feel good, it’s like it doesn’t really reach me the way it used to. Preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those used to really stir something in me. Now I mostly just go through the motions, or I avoid it altogether, because it doesn’t feel alive in me anymore.\n\nDoes that make sense? Like… I know something is supposed to be good, but I don’t really feel it strong.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you have completely stopped doing since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say completely with everything, but there are things I’ve pretty much stopped doing unless I have to.\n\nLike taking pictures — I don’t really pick up the camera the way I used to. And playing keys… I might sit down sometimes, but it’s not like before. Same with getting on the court with the guys. I’ll tell myself I’m busy or just not show up.\n\nPreaching is harder to say because I still have responsibilities, but even there, a lot of the time I’m just going through the motions. None of it really feels alive in me anymore. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've significantly reduced or lost interest in since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve just quit everything, because I still try to show up where I’m supposed to.\n\nBut preaching used to mean a lot to me, and playing keys too. Taking pictures, running with the guys on the court… those were things I actually looked forward to. Since my cousin died, I’ve pulled back from a lot of it. Or if I do it, I’m kind of just there, going through the motions.\n\nIt just doesn’t feel alive in me like it used to. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to find very enjoyable that you've lost most of your interest in since your cousin passed away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that’s fair to say.\n\nI used to really love preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, and getting out on the court with the guys. Those things used to give me something, you know? Like they felt meaningful, or fun, or just… alive.\n\nNow I either avoid them, or if I do show up, I’m mostly just going through the motions. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care about anything, but it’s just not there the same way anymore. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling guilty or blaming yourself for what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: I guess… pretty much since it happened.\n\nI don’t like saying it like that, because I know maybe I shouldn’t put it all on myself, but that guilt’s been there since my cousin was killed — so… about two years and three months now. It’s kind of stayed with me that whole time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, thank you for seeing me. I guess I’ve been trying to handle it, and maybe it’s not as bad as I’m making it sound, but… I keep getting these moments where I’m just suddenly back there.\n\nIt’s the scene with my cousin on the pavement. I can hear the shouting again, see the lights, like it’s happening right in front of me—even if I’m just standing in the church office trying to act normal. It kind of hits me out of nowhere. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these sudden moments of being back at the scene with your cousin?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much since it happened. My cousin was killed about two years and three months ago now, and these moments started after that. I guess I kept thinking they’d fade if I just stayed busy, but they’re still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities because they remind you of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make a big deal out of it, but I guess I do.\n\nI’ll take the long way around town so I don’t have to pass the community center, or that court nearby. It probably sounds kind of silly, but if I’m already having a hard day, I just… I don’t want to see it.\n\nAnd if people start talking about that night, I usually shut it down pretty quickly or find some reason to step out. I try to be polite about it, but I just don’t want to go there.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep sometimes. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nSome nights are fine enough, I guess, and other nights I’m just… not really settled. But I don’t know if I’d call it “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean. Is there a specific kind of sleep problem you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself jumping or being easily startled by loud noises or unexpected movements?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really paid attention to, anyway.\n\nI mean, loud noises aren’t my favorite, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m jumping out of my skin or anything. Maybe if something is really sudden, like anyone would react, but I haven’t thought of it as a major thing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know logically people tell me it wasn’t my fault. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with that.\n\nBut I do go over it a lot. I keep thinking if I had ended things earlier, or walked him out myself, or just kept a better eye on those boys… maybe he’d still be here.\n\nI know that may not be fair to say, but it’s hard not to feel like I missed something I was supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I’m worthless exactly. That sounds harsh, and I know I’m not supposed to talk about myself that way.\n\nBut most days there’s this… weight in my chest, like fear and anger and guilt all mixed together. From the time I wake up, I feel like I failed God, and my family, and the kids who trusted me. I know maybe that’s not the same as being a bad person, but it feels close sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to feel happy or loving feelings towards people or things you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care about people or anything, because I do.\n\nBut things I used to really love just don’t feel the same. Preaching used to feel… alive to me. Playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those were things that filled me up. Now I either avoid them or I just kind of do them because I’m supposed to.\n\nIt’s like I’m there, but I’m not really in it. I don’t know if that answers it right, but… yeah, it’s harder to feel that joy like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your cousin's death that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not really like before.\n\nI mean, I still try sometimes. I’ll sit at the keys or show up around the guys, or I’ll do what I need to do with preaching, but it doesn’t feel alive in me the way it used to. Taking pictures, playing, being on the court… those used to give me something back.\n\nNow a lot of times I either avoid it, or I’m there but I’m just kind of going through the motions. I wish I could say there’s one thing that still feels the same, but… I don’t know. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to feel any strong emotions at all, even when something good happens?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I can’t feel anything at all… I mean, that sounds pretty extreme, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut even when something should feel good, it’s like it doesn’t really reach me the way it used to. Preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those used to really stir something in me. Now I mostly just go through the motions, or I avoid it altogether, because it doesn’t feel alive in me anymore.\n\nDoes that make sense? Like… I know something is supposed to be good, but I don’t really feel it strong.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you have completely stopped doing since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say completely with everything, but there are things I’ve pretty much stopped doing unless I have to.\n\nLike taking pictures — I don’t really pick up the camera the way I used to. And playing keys… I might sit down sometimes, but it’s not like before. Same with getting on the court with the guys. I’ll tell myself I’m busy or just not show up.\n\nPreaching is harder to say because I still have responsibilities, but even there, a lot of the time I’m just going through the motions. None of it really feels alive in me anymore. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've significantly reduced or lost interest in since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve just quit everything, because I still try to show up where I’m supposed to.\n\nBut preaching used to mean a lot to me, and playing keys too. Taking pictures, running with the guys on the court… those were things I actually looked forward to. Since my cousin died, I’ve pulled back from a lot of it. Or if I do it, I’m kind of just there, going through the motions.\n\nIt just doesn’t feel alive in me like it used to. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to find very enjoyable that you've lost most of your interest in since your cousin passed away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that’s fair to say.\n\nI used to really love preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, and getting out on the court with the guys. Those things used to give me something, you know? Like they felt meaningful, or fun, or just… alive.\n\nNow I either avoid them, or if I do show up, I’m mostly just going through the motions. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling guilty or blaming yourself for what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: I guess… pretty much since it happened.\n\nI don’t like saying it like that, because I know maybe I shouldn’t put it all on myself, but that guilt’s been there since my cousin was killed — so… about two years and three months now. Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, thank you for seeing me. I guess I’ve been trying to handle it, and maybe it’s not as bad as I’m making it sound, but… I keep getting these moments where I’m just suddenly back there.\n\nIt’s the scene with my cousin on the pavement. I can hear the shouting again, see the lights, like it’s happening right in front of me—even if I’m just standing in the church office trying to act normal. It kind of hits me out of nowhere. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these sudden moments of being back at the scene with your cousin?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much since it happened. My cousin was killed about two years and three months ago now, and these moments started after that. I guess I kept thinking they’d fade if I just stayed busy, but they’re still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities because they remind you of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make a big deal out of it, but I guess I do.\n\nI’ll take the long way around town so I don’t have to pass the community center, or that court nearby. It probably sounds kind of silly, but if I’m already having a hard day, I just… I don’t want to see it.\n\nAnd if people start talking about that night, I usually shut it down pretty quickly or find some reason to step out. I try to be polite about it, but I just don’t want to go there.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep sometimes. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nSome nights are fine enough, I guess, and other nights I’m just… not really settled. But I don’t know if I’d call it “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean. Is there a specific kind of sleep problem you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself jumping or being easily startled by loud noises or unexpected movements?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really paid attention to, anyway.\n\nI mean, loud noises aren’t my favorite, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m jumping out of my skin or anything. Maybe if something is really sudden, like anyone would react, but I haven’t thought of it as a major thing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know logically people tell me it wasn’t my fault. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with that.\n\nBut I do go over it a lot. I keep thinking if I had ended things earlier, or walked him out myself, or just kept a better eye on those boys… maybe he’d still be here.\n\nI know that may not be fair to say, but it’s hard not to feel like I missed something I was supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I’m worthless exactly. That sounds harsh, and I know I’m not supposed to talk about myself that way.\n\nBut most days there’s this… weight in my chest, like fear and anger and guilt all mixed together. From the time I wake up, I feel like I failed God, and my family, and the kids who trusted me. I know maybe that’s not the same as being a bad person, but it feels close sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to feel happy or loving feelings towards people or things you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care about people or anything, because I do.\n\nBut things I used to really love just don’t feel the same. Preaching used to feel… alive to me. Playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those were things that filled me up. Now I either avoid them or I just kind of do them because I’m supposed to.\n\nIt’s like I’m there, but I’m not really in it. I don’t know if that answers it right, but… yeah, it’s harder to feel that joy like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your cousin's death that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not really like before.\n\nI mean, I still try sometimes. I’ll sit at the keys or show up around the guys, or I’ll do what I need to do with preaching, but it doesn’t feel alive in me the way it used to. Taking pictures, playing, being on the court… those used to give me something back.\n\nNow a lot of times I either avoid it, or I’m there but I’m just kind of going through the motions. I wish I could say there’s one thing that still feels the same, but… I don’t know. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to feel any strong emotions at all, even when something good happens?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I can’t feel anything at all… I mean, that sounds pretty extreme, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut even when something should feel good, it’s like it doesn’t really reach me the way it used to. Preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those used to really stir something in me. Now I mostly just go through the motions, or I avoid it altogether, because it doesn’t feel alive in me anymore.\n\nDoes that make sense? Like… I know something is supposed to be good, but I don’t really feel it strong.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you have completely stopped doing since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say completely with everything, but there are things I’ve pretty much stopped doing unless I have to.\n\nLike taking pictures — I don’t really pick up the camera the way I used to. And playing keys… I might sit down sometimes, but it’s not like before. Same with getting on the court with the guys. I’ll tell myself I’m busy or just not show up.\n\nPreaching is harder to say because I still have responsibilities, but even there, a lot of the time I’m just going through the motions. None of it really feels alive in me anymore. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've significantly reduced or lost interest in since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve just quit everything, because I still try to show up where I’m supposed to.\n\nBut preaching used to mean a lot to me, and playing keys too. Taking pictures, running with the guys on the court… those were things I actually looked forward to. Since my cousin died, I’ve pulled back from a lot of it. Or if I do it, I’m kind of just there, going through the motions.\n\nIt just doesn’t feel alive in me like it used to. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to find very enjoyable that you've lost most of your interest in since your cousin passed away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that’s fair to say.\n\nI used to really love preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, and getting out on the court with the guys. I know maybe that’s not the same as being a bad person, but it feels close sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to feel happy or loving feelings towards people or things you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care about people or anything, because I do.\n\nBut things I used to really love just don’t feel the same. Preaching used to feel… alive to me. Playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those were things that filled me up. Now I either avoid them or I just kind of do them because I’m supposed to.\n\nIt’s like I’m there, but I’m not really in it. I don’t know if that answers it right, but… yeah, it’s harder to feel that joy like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your cousin's death that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not really like before.\n\nI mean, I still try sometimes. I’ll sit at the keys or show up around the guys, or I’ll do what I need to do with preaching, but it doesn’t feel alive in me the way it used to. Taking pictures, playing, being on the court… those used to give me something back.\n\nNow a lot of times I either avoid it, or I’m there but I’m just kind of going through the motions. I wish I could say there’s one thing that still feels the same, but… I don’t know. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to feel any strong emotions at all, even when something good happens?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I can’t feel anything at all… I mean, that sounds pretty extreme, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut even when something should feel good, it’s like it doesn’t really reach me the way it used to. Preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court — those used to really stir something in me. Now I mostly just go through the motions, or I avoid it altogether, because it doesn’t feel alive in me anymore.\n\nDoes that make sense? Like… I know something is supposed to be good, but I don’t really feel it strong.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you have completely stopped doing since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say completely with everything, but there are things I’ve pretty much stopped doing unless I have to.\n\nLike taking pictures — I don’t really pick up the camera the way I used to. I’ll tell myself I’m busy or just not show up.\n\nPreaching is harder to say because I still have responsibilities, but even there, a lot of the time I’m just going through the motions. None of it really feels alive in me anymore. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've significantly reduced or lost interest in since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to find very enjoyable that you've lost most of your interest in since your cousin passed away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that’s fair to say.\n\nI used to really love preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, and getting out on the court with the guys. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care about anything, but it’s just not there the same way anymore. It doesn’t feel alive in me the way it used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find very enjoyable, but now you have almost no interest in them at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes.\n\nPreaching used to feel really alive to me. I keep thinking if I had ended things earlier, or walked him out myself, or just kept a better eye on those boys… maybe he’d still be here.\n\nI know that may not be fair to say, but it’s hard not to feel like I missed something I was supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I’m worthless exactly. Like… I know something is supposed to be good, but I don’t really feel it strong.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you have completely stopped doing since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say completely with everything, but there are things I’ve pretty much stopped doing unless I have to.\n\nLike taking pictures — I don’t really pick up the camera the way I used to. None of it really feels alive in me anymore. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve just quit everything, because I still try to show up where I’m supposed to.\n\nBut preaching used to mean a lot to me, and playing keys too.
